This enables\n    frameworks built on top of Ginkgo to modify Ginkgo nodes and enforce\n    conventions.\n    \n    Learn more\n    [here](https://onsi.github.io/ginkgo/#advanced-transforming-node-arguments-during-tree-construction).\n    \n    ##### Spec Prioritization\n    \n    A new `SpecPriority(int)` decorator has been added. Ginkgo will honor\n    priority when ordering specs, ensuring that higher priority specs start\n    running before lower priority specs\n    \n    Learn more [here](https://onsi.github.io/ginkgo/#prioritizing-specs).\n    \n    ##### Maintenance\n    \n    - Bump rexml from 3.4.0 to 3.4.2 in /docs\n    ([#&#8203;1595](https://redirect.github.com/onsi/ginkgo/issues/1595))\n    \\[[`1333dae`](https://redirect.github.com/onsi/ginkgo/commit/1333dae)]\n    - Bump github.com/gkampitakis/go-snaps from 0.5.14 to 0.5.15\n    ([#&#8203;1600](https://redirect.github.com/onsi/ginkgo/issues/1600))\n    \\[[`17ae63e`](https://redirect.github.com/onsi/ginkgo/commit/17ae63e)]\n    \n    ###\n    [`v2.26.0`](https://redirect.github.com/onsi/ginkgo/releases/tag/v2.26.0)\n    \n    [Compare\n    Source](https://redirect.github.com/onsi/ginkgo/compare/v2.25.3...v2.26.0)\n    \n    #### 2.26.0\n    \n    ##### Features\n    \n    Ginkgo can now generate json-formatted reports that are compatible with\n    the `go test` json format. Use `ginkgo --gojson-report=report.go.json`.\n    This is not intended to be a replacement for Ginkgo's native json format\n    which is more information rich and better models Ginkgo's test structure\n    semantics.\n    \n    ###\n    [`v2.25.3`](https://redirect.github.com/onsi/ginkgo/releases/tag/v2.25.3)\n    \n    [Compare\n    Source](https://redirect.github.com/onsi/ginkgo/compare/v2.25.2...v2.25.3)\n    \n    #### 2.25.3\n    \n    ##### Fixes\n    \n    - fix(types): ignore nameless nodes on FullText()\n    \\[[`10866d3`](https://redirect.github.com/onsi/ginkgo/commit/10866d3)]\n    - chore: fix some CodeQL warnings\n    \\[[`2e42cff`](https://redirect.github.com/onsi/ginkgo/commit/2e42cff)]\n    \n    ###\n    [`v2.25.0`](https://redirect.github.com/onsi/ginkgo/releases/tag/v2.25.0)\n    \n    [Compare\n    Source](https://redirect.github.com/onsi/ginkgo/compare/v2.24.0...v2.25.0)\n    \n    #### 2.25.0\n    \n    ##### `AroundNode`\n    \n    This release introduces a new decorator to support more complex spec\n    setup usecases.\n    \n    `AroundNode` registers a function that runs before each individual node.\n    This is considered a more advanced decorator.\n    \n    Please read the\n    [docs](https://onsi.github.io/ginkgo/#advanced-around-node) for more\n    information and some examples.\n    \n    Allowed signatures:\n    \n    - `AroundNode(func())` - `func` will be called before the node is run.\n    - `AroundNode(func(ctx context.Context) context.Context)` - `func` can\n    wrap the passed in context and return a new one which will be passed on\n    to the node.\n    - `AroundNode(func(ctx context.Context, body func(ctx\n    context.Context)))` - `ctx` is the context for the node and `body` is a\n    function that must be called to run the node. This gives you complete\n    control over what runs before and after the node.\n    \n    Multiple `AroundNode` decorators can be applied to a single node and\n    they will run in the order they are applied.\n    \n    Unlike setup nodes like `BeforeEach` and `DeferCleanup`, `AroundNode` is\n    guaranteed to run in the same goroutine as the decorated node. This is\n    necessary when working with lower-level libraries that must run on a\n    single thread (you can call `runtime.LockOSThread()` in the `AroundNode`\n    to ensure that the node runs on a single thread).\n    \n    Since `AroundNode` allows you to modify the context you can also use\n    `AroundNode` to implement shared setup that attaches values to the\n    context.\n    \n    If applied to a container, `AroundNode` will run before every node in\n    the container. Including setup nodes like `BeforeEach` and\n    `DeferCleanup`.\n    \n    `AroundNode` can also be applied to `RunSpecs` to run before every node\n    in the suite. This opens up new mechanisms for instrumenting individual\n    nodes across an entire suite.\n    \n    ###\n    [`v2.24.0`](https://redirect.github.com/onsi/ginkgo/releases/tag/v2.24.0)\n    \n    [Compare\n    Source](https://redirect.github.com/onsi/ginkgo/compare/v2.23.4...v2.24.0)\n    \n    #### 2.24.0\n    \n    ##### Features\n    \n    Specs can now be decorated with (e.g.) `SemVerConstraint(\"2.1.0\")` and\n    `ginkgo --sem-ver-filter=\"2.1.1\"` will only run constrained specs that\n    match the requested version. Learn more in the docs\n    [here](https://onsi.github.io/ginkgo/#spec-semantic-version-filtering)!\n    Thanks to [@&#8203;Icarus9913](https://redirect.github.com/Icarus9913)\n    for the PR.\n    \n    ##### Fixes\n    \n    - remove -o from run command\n    \\[[`3f5d379`](https://redirect.github.com/onsi/ginkgo/commit/3f5d379)].\n    fixes\n    [#&#8203;1582](https://redirect.github.com/onsi/ginkgo/issues/1582)\n    \n    ##### Maintenance\n    \n    Numerous dependency bumps and documentation fixes\n    \n    ###\n    [`v2.23.4`](https://redirect.github.com/onsi/ginkgo/releases/tag/v2.23.4)\n    \n    [Compare\n    Source](https://redirect.github.com/onsi/ginkgo/compare/v2.23.3...v2.23.4)\n    \n    #### 2.23.4\n    \n    Prior to this release Ginkgo would compute the incorrect number of\n    available CPUs when running with `-p` in a linux container. <details>\n    <summary>stretchr/testify (github.com/stretchr/testify)</summary>\n    \n    ###\n    [`v1.11.1`](https://redirect.github.com/stretchr/testify/releases/tag/v1.11.1)\n    \n    [Compare\n    Source](https://redirect.github.com/stretchr/testify/compare/v1.11.0...v1.11.1)\n    \n    This release fixes\n    [#&#8203;1785](https://redirect.github.com/stretchr/testify/issues/1785)\n    introduced in v1.11.0 where expected argument values implementing the\n    stringer interface (`String() string`) with a method which mutates their\n    value, when passed to mock.Mock.On (`m.On(\"Method\",\n    <expected>).Return()`) or actual argument values passed to\n    mock.Mock.Called may no longer match one another where they previously\n    did match. The behaviour prior to v1.11.0 where the stringer is always\n    called is restored. Future testify releases may not call the stringer\n    method at all in this case.\n    \n    #### What's Changed\n    \n    - Backport\n    [#&#8203;1786](https://redirect.github.com/stretchr/testify/issues/1786)\n    to release/1.11: mock: revert to pre-v1.11.0 argument matching behavior\n    for mutating stringers by\n    [@&#8203;brackendawson](https://redirect.github.com/brackendawson) in\n    [#&#8203;1788](https://redirect.github.com/stretchr/testify/pull/1788)\n    \n    **Full Changelog**:\n    <https://github.com/stretchr/testify/compare/v1.11.0...v1.11.1>\n    \n    ###\n    [`v1.11.0`](https://redirect.github.com/stretchr/testify/releases/tag/v1.11.0)\n    \n    [Compare\n    Source](https://redirect.github.com/stretchr/testify/compare/v1.10.0...v1.11.0)\n    \n    #### What's Changed\n    \n    ##### Functional Changes\n    \n    v1.11.0 Includes a number of performance improvements.\n    \n    - Now it prints an\n    error\n    [#&#8203;31578](https://redirect.github.com/helm/helm/issues/31578)\n    - Fixed a bug where OCI references with tag+digest failed with \"invalid\n    byte\" error\n    [#&#8203;31601](https://redirect.github.com/helm/helm/issues/31601)\n    - Fixed a bug where user-provided nil value was not preserved when chart\n    has an empty map or no default for a key\n    [#&#8203;31644](https://redirect.github.com/helm/helm/issues/31644)\n    - Fixed a regression since Helm 3.18.0 where Pulling charts from OCI\n    repositories that use an index to store both Container Images and Helm\n    Charts under the same tag failed\n    [#&#8203;31776](https://redirect.github.com/helm/helm/issues/31776)\n    - Fixed a Helm 4 regression where gotemplate white space trimming\n    directly after YAML doc separators, when present after postrendering,\n    caused YAML file corruption\n    [#&#8203;31868](https://redirect.github.com/helm/helm/issues/31868)\n    - Fixed a bug where `FailedStatus` is treated as a terminal state,\n    causing upgrades to fail prematurely when cluster autoscalers needed\n    time to provision nodes, or when pods were being deleted during rolling\n    updates\n    [#&#8203;31897](https://redirect.github.com/helm/helm/issues/31897)\n    - Fixed broken backwards compatibility for deprecated `--atomic` flag on\n    install command\n    [#&#8203;31901](https://redirect.github.com/helm/helm/issues/31901)\n    - SDK: Fixed a Windows 'Access Deined' error if multiple processes try\n    to download the same chart version concurrently\n    [#&#8203;31128](https://redirect.github.com/helm/helm/issues/31128)\n    - SDK: Fixed a bug where users did not receive any logs from the waiter,\n    causing confusion as several minutes could pass with no user feedback\n    [#&#8203;31717](https://redirect.github.com/helm/helm/issues/31717)\n    - SDK: Fixed a bug where server-side apply defaults did not always match\n    the CLI defaults\n    [#&#8203;31732](https://redirect.github.com/helm/helm/issues/31732)\n    - SDK: Fixed a Go import issue when downstream tooling attempted to\n    vendor helm.sh/helm/v4/pkg/kube\n    [#&#8203;31852](https://redirect.github.com/helm/helm/issues/31852)\n    \n    #### Installation and Upgrading\n    \n    Download Helm v4.1.3. This auto-updated the required version of Go to\n    1.24, consistent with the fact that Go 1.23 has been out of support for\n    almost six months.\n    \n    ###\n    [`v2.28.0`](https://redirect.github.com/onsi/ginkgo/releases/tag/v2.28.0)\n    \n    [Compare\n    Source](https://redirect.github.com/onsi/ginkgo/compare/v2.27.5...v2.28.0)\n    \n    #### 2.28.0\n    \n    Ginkgo's SemVer filter now supports filtering multiple components by\n    SemVer version:\n    \n    ```go\n    It(\"should work in a specific version range (1.0.0, 2.0.0) and third-party dependency redis in [8.0.0, ~)\", SemVerConstraint(\">= 3.2.0\"), ComponentSemVerConstraint(\"redis\", \">= 8.0.0\") func() {\n        // This test will only run when version is between 1.0.0 (exclusive) and 2.0.0 (exclusive) and redis version is >= 8.0.0\n    })\n    ```\n    \n    can be filtered in or out with an invocation like:\n    \n    ```bash\n    ginkgo --sem-ver-filter=\"2.1.1, redis=8.2.0\"\n    ```\n    \n    Huge thanks to\n    [@&#8203;Icarus9913](https://redirect.github.com/Icarus9913) for working\n    on this!\n    \n    ###\n    [`v2.27.5`](https://redirect.github.com/onsi/ginkgo/releases/tag/v2.27.5)\n    \n    [Compare\n    Source](https://redirect.github.com/onsi/ginkgo/compare/v2.27.4...v2.27.5)\n    \n    #### 2.27.5\n    \n    ##### Fixes\n    \n    Don't make a new formatter for each GinkgoT(); This enables\n    frameworks built on top of Ginkgo to modify Ginkgo nodes and enforce\n    conventions.\n    \n    Learn more\n    [here](https://onsi.github.io/ginkgo/#advanced-transforming-node-arguments-during-tree-construction).\n    \n    ##### Spec Prioritization\n    \n    A new `SpecPriority(int)` decorator has been added. Ginkgo will honor\n    priority when ordering specs, ensuring that higher priority specs start\n    running before lower priority specs\n    \n    Learn more [here](https://onsi.github.io/ginkgo/#prioritizing-specs).\n    \n    ##### Maintenance\n    \n    - Bump rexml from 3.4.0 to 3.4.2 in /docs\n    ([#&#8203;1595](https://redirect.github.com/onsi/ginkgo/issues/1595))\n    \\[[`1333dae`](https://redirect.github.com/onsi/ginkgo/commit/1333dae)]\n    - Bump github.com/gkampitakis/go-snaps from 0.5.14 to 0.5.15\n    ([#&#8203;1600](https://redirect.github.com/onsi/ginkgo/issues/1600))\n    \\[[`17ae63e`](https://redirect.github.com/onsi/ginkgo/commit/17ae63e)]\n    \n    ###\n    [`v2.26.0`](https://redirect.github.com/onsi/ginkgo/releases/tag/v2.26.0)\n    \n    [Compare\n    Source](https://redirect.github.com/onsi/ginkgo/compare/v2.25.3...v2.26.0)\n    \n    #### 2.26.0\n    \n    ##### Features\n    \n    Ginkgo can now generate json-formatted reports that are compatible with\n    the `go test` json format. Use `ginkgo --gojson-report=report.go.json`.\n    This is not intended to be a replacement for Ginkgo's native json format\n    which is more information rich and better models Ginkgo's test structure\n    semantics.\n    \n    ###\n    [`v2.25.3`](https://redirect.github.com/onsi/ginkgo/releases/tag/v2.25.3)\n    \n    [Compare\n    Source](https://redirect.github.com/onsi/ginkgo/compare/v2.25.2...v2.25.3)\n    \n    #### 2.25.3\n    \n    ##### Fixes\n    \n    - fix(types): ignore nameless nodes on FullText()\n    \\[[`10866d3`](https://redirect.github.com/onsi/ginkgo/commit/10866d3)]\n    - chore: fix some CodeQL warnings\n    \\[[`2e42cff`](https://redirect.github.com/onsi/ginkgo/commit/2e42cff)]\n    \n    ###\n    [`v2.25.0`](https://redirect.github.com/onsi/ginkgo/releases/tag/v2.25.0)\n    \n    [Compare\n    Source](https://redirect.github.com/onsi/ginkgo/compare/v2.24.0...v2.25.0)\n    \n    #### 2.25.0\n    \n    ##### `AroundNode`\n    \n    This release introduces a new decorator to support more complex spec\n    setup usecases.\n    \n    `AroundNode` registers a function that runs before each individual node.\n    This is considered a more advanced decorator.\n    \n    Please read the\n    [docs](https://onsi.github.io/ginkgo/#advanced-around-node) for more\n    information and some examples.\n    \n    Allowed signatures:\n    \n    - `AroundNode(func())` - `func` will be called before the node is run.\n    - `AroundNode(func(ctx context.Context) context.Context)` - `func` can\n    wrap the passed in context and return a new one which will be passed on\n    to the node.\n    - `AroundNode(func(ctx context.Context, body func(ctx\n    context.Context)))` - `ctx` is the context for the node and `body` is a\n    function that must be called to run the node. This gives you complete\n    control over what runs before and after the node.\n    \n    Multiple `AroundNode` decorators can be applied to a single node and\n    they will run in the order they are applied.\n    \n    Unlike setup nodes like `BeforeEach` and `DeferCleanup`, `AroundNode` is\n    guaranteed to run in the same goroutine as the decorated node. This is\n    necessary when working with lower-level libraries that must run on a\n    single thread (you can call `runtime.LockOSThread()` in the `AroundNode`\n    to ensure that the node runs on a single thread).\n    \n    Since `AroundNode` allows you to modify the context you can also use\n    `AroundNode` to implement shared setup that attaches values to the\n    context.\n    \n    If applied to a container, `AroundNode` will run before every node in\n    the container. Including setup nodes like `BeforeEach` and\n    `DeferCleanup`.\n    \n    `AroundNode` can also be applied to `RunSpecs` to run before every node\n    in the suite. This opens up new mechanisms for instrumenting individual\n    nodes across an entire suite.\n    \n    ###\n    [`v2.24.0`](https://redirect.github.com/onsi/ginkgo/releases/tag/v2.24.0)\n    \n    [Compare\n    Source](https://redirect.github.com/onsi/ginkgo/compare/v2.23.4...v2.24.0)\n    \n    #### 2.24.0\n    \n    ##### Features\n    \n    Specs can now be decorated with (e.g.) `SemVerConstraint(\"2.1.0\")` and\n    `ginkgo --sem-ver-filter=\"2.1.1\"` will only run constrained specs that\n    match the requested version. Learn more in the docs\n    [here](https://onsi.github.io/ginkgo/#spec-semantic-version-filtering)!\n    Thanks to [@&#8203;Icarus9913](https://redirect.github.com/Icarus9913)\n    for the PR.\n    \n    ##### Fixes\n    \n    - remove -o from run command\n    \\[[`3f5d379`](https://redirect.github.com/onsi/ginkgo/commit/3f5d379)].\n    fixes\n    [#&#8203;1582](https://redirect.github.com/onsi/ginkgo/issues/1582)\n    \n    ##### Maintenance\n    \n    Numerous dependency bumps and documentation fixes\n    \n    ###\n    [`v2.23.4`](https://redirect.github.com/onsi/ginkgo/releases/tag/v2.23.4)\n    \n    [Compare\n    Source](https://redirect.github.com/onsi/ginkgo/compare/v2.23.3...v2.23.4)\n    \n    #### 2.23.4\n    \n    Prior to this release Ginkgo would compute the incorrect number of\n    available CPUs when running with `-p` in a linux container. <details>\n    <summary>stretchr/testify (github.com/stretchr/testify)</summary>\n    \n    ###\n    [`v1.11.1`](https://redirect.github.com/stretchr/testify/releases/tag/v1.11.1)\n    \n    [Compare\n    Source](https://redirect.github.com/stretchr/testify/compare/v1.11.0...v1.11.1)\n    \n    This release fixes\n    [#&#8203;1785](https://redirect.github.com/stretchr/testify/issues/1785)\n    introduced in v1.11.0 where expected argument values implementing the\n    stringer interface (`String() string`) with a method which mutates their\n    value, when passed to mock.Mock.On (`m.On(\"Method\",\n    <expected>).Return()`) or actual argument values passed to\n    mock.Mock.Called may no longer match one another where they previously\n    did match. The behaviour prior to v1.11.0 where the stringer is always\n    called is restored. Future testify releases may not call the stringer\n    method at all in this case.\n    \n    #### What's Changed\n    \n    - Backport\n    [#&#8203;1786](https://redirect.github.com/stretchr/testify/issues/1786)\n    to release/1.11: mock: revert to pre-v1.11.0 argument matching behavior\n    for mutating stringers by\n    [@&#8203;brackendawson](https://redirect.github.com/brackendawson) in\n    [#&#8203;1788](https://redirect.github.com/stretchr/testify/pull/1788)\n    \n    **Full Changelog**:\n    <https://github.com/stretchr/testify/compare/v1.11.0...v1.11.1>\n    \n    ###\n    [`v1.11.0`](https://redirect.github.com/stretchr/testify/releases/tag/v1.11.0)\n    \n    [Compare\n    Source](https://redirect.github.com/stretchr/testify/compare/v1.10.0...v1.11.0)\n    \n    #### What's Changed\n    \n    ##### Functional Changes\n    \n    v1.11.0 Includes a number of performance improvements.\n    \n    - 19:31:39 2026 +0000\n\n    chore(deps): Update module github.com/go-viper/mapstructure/v2 to v2.4.0 [SECURITY] (#26)\n    \n    This PR contains the following updates:\n    \n    | Package | Change |\n    [Age](https://docs.renovatebot.com/merge-confidence/) |\n    [Confidence](https://docs.renovatebot.com/merge-confidence/) |\n    |---|---|---|---|\n    |\n    [github.com/go-viper/mapstructure/v2](https://redirect.github.com/go-viper/mapstructure)\n    | `v2.2.1` → `v2.4.0` |\n    ![age](https://developer.mend.io/api/mc/badges/age/go/github.com%2fgo-viper%2fmapstructure%2fv2/v2.4.0?slim=true)\n    |\n    ![confidence](https://developer.mend.io/api/mc/badges/confidence/go/github.com%2fgo-viper%2fmapstructure%2fv2/v2.2.1/v2.4.0?slim=true)\n    |\n    \n    ### GitHub Vulnerability Alerts\n    \n    ####\n    [GHSA-fv92-fjc5-jj9h](https://redirect.github.com/go-viper/mapstructure/security/advisories/GHSA-fv92-fjc5-jj9h)\n    \n    ### Summary\n    \n    Use of this library in a security-critical context may result in leaking\n    sensitive information, if used to process sensitive fields.\n    \n    ### Details\n    \n    OpenBao (and presumably HashiCorp Vault) have surfaced error messages\n    from `mapstructure` as follows:\n    \n    \n    https://github.com/openbao/openbao/blob/98c3a59c040efca724353ca46ca79bd5cdbab920/sdk/framework/field_data.go#L43-L50\n    \n    ```go\n                            _, _, err := d.getPrimitive(field, schema)\n                            if err != nil {\n                                    return fmt.Errorf(\"error converting input for field %q: %w\", field, err)\n                            }\n    ```\n    \n    where this calls `mapstructure.WeakDecode(...)`:\n    https://github.com/openbao/openbao/blob/98c3a59c040efca724353ca46ca79bd5cdbab920/sdk/framework/field_data.go#L181-L193\n    \n    ```go\n    \n    func (d *FieldData) getPrimitive(k string, schema *FieldSchema) (interface{}, bool, error) {\n            raw, ok := d.Raw[k]\n            if !ok {\n                    return nil, false, nil\n            }\n    \n            switch t := schema.Type; t {\n            case TypeBool:\n                    var result bool\n                    if err := mapstructure.WeakDecode(raw, &result); err != nil {\n                            return nil, false, err\n                    }\n                    return result, true, nil\n    ```\n    \n    Notably, `WeakDecode(...)` eventually calls one of the decode helpers,\n    which surfaces the original value:\n    \n    \n    https://github.com/go-viper/mapstructure/blob/1a66224d5e54d8757f63bd66339cf764c3292c21/mapstructure.go#L679-L686\n    \n    \n    https://github.com/go-viper/mapstructure/blob/1a66224d5e54d8757f63bd66339cf764c3292c21/mapstructure.go#L726-L730\n    \n    \n    https://github.com/go-viper/mapstructure/blob/1a66224d5e54d8757f63bd66339cf764c3292c21/mapstructure.go#L783-L787\n    \n    & more.\n    \n    ### PoC\n    \n    To reproduce with OpenBao:\n    \n    ```\n    $ podman run -p 8300:8300 openbao/openbao:latest server -dev -dev-root-token-id=root -dev-listen-address=0.0.0.0:8300\n    ```\n    \n    and in a new tab:\n    \n    ```\n    $ BAO_TOKEN=root BAO_ADDR=http://localhost:8300 bao auth enable userpass\n    Success! Enabled userpass auth method at: userpass/\n    $ curl -X PUT -H \"X-Vault-Request: true\" -H \"X-Vault-Token: root\" -d '{\"password\":{\"asdf\":\"my-sensitive-value\"}}' \"http://localhost:8300/v1/auth/userpass/users/adsf\"\n    {\"errors\":[\"error converting input for field \\\"password\\\": '' expected type 'string', got unconvertible type 'map[string]interface {}', value: 'map[asdf:my-sensitive-value]'\"]}\n    ```\n    \n    ### Impact\n    \n    This is an information disclosure bug with little mitigation. See\n    https://discuss.hashicorp.com/t/hcsec-2025-09-vault-may-expose-sensitive-information-in-error-logs-when-processing-malformed-data-with-the-kv-v2-plugin/74717\n    for a previous version. That version was fixed, but this is in the\n    second part of that error message (starting at `'' expected a map, got\n    'string'` -- when the field type is `string` and a `map` is provided, we\n    see the above information leak -- the previous example had a `map` type\n    field with a `string` value provided).\n    \n    This was rated 4.5 Medium by HashiCorp in the past iteration.\n    \n    ####\n    [CVE-2025-11065](https://redirect.github.com/go-viper/mapstructure/security/advisories/GHSA-2464-8j7c-4cjm)\n    \n    ### Summary\n    \n    Use of this library in a security-critical context may result in leaking\n    sensitive information, if used to process sensitive fields.\n    \n    ### Details\n    \n    OpenBao (and presumably HashiCorp Vault) have surfaced error messages\n    from `mapstructure` as follows:\n    \n    \n    https://github.com/openbao/openbao/blob/98c3a59c040efca724353ca46ca79bd5cdbab920/sdk/framework/field_data.go#L43-L50\n    \n    ```go\n                            _, _, err := d.getPrimitive(field, schema)\n                            if err != nil {\n                                    return fmt.Errorf(\"error converting input for field %q: %w\", field, err)\n                            }\n    ```\n    \n    where this calls `mapstructure.WeakDecode(...)`:\n    https://github.com/openbao/openbao/blob/98c3a59c040efca724353ca46ca79bd5cdbab920/sdk/framework/field_data.go#L181-L193\n    \n    ```go\n    \n    func (d *FieldData) getPrimitive(k string, schema *FieldSchema) (interface{}, bool, error) {\n            raw, ok := d.Raw[k]\n            if !ok {\n                    return nil, false, nil\n            }\n    \n            switch t := schema.Type; t {\n            case TypeBool:\n                    var result bool\n                    if err := mapstructure.WeakDecode(raw, &result); err != nil {\n                            return nil, false, err\n                    }\n                    return result, true, nil\n    ```\n    \n    Notably, `WeakDecode(...)` eventually calls one of the decode helpers,\n    which surfaces the original value via `strconv` helpers:\n    \n    \n    https://github.com/go-viper/mapstructure/blob/8c61ec1924fcfa522f9fc6b4618c672db61d1a38/mapstructure.go#L720-L727\n    \n    \n    https://github.com/go-viper/mapstructure/blob/8c61ec1924fcfa522f9fc6b4618c672db61d1a38/mapstructure.go#L791-L798\n    \n    \n    https://github.com/go-viper/mapstructure/blob/8c61ec1924fcfa522f9fc6b4618c672db61d1a38/decode_hooks.go#L180\n    \n    & more. These are different code paths than are fixed in the previous\n    iteration at\n    https://github.com/go-viper/mapstructure/security/advisories/GHSA-fv92-fjc5-jj9h.\n    \n    ### PoC\n    \n    To reproduce with OpenBao:\n    \n    ```\n    $ podman run --pull=always -p 8300:8300 openbao/openbao:latest server -dev -dev-root-token-id=root -dev-listen-address=0.0.0.0:8300\n    ```\n    \n    and in a new tab:\n    \n    ```\n    $ BAO_TOKEN=root BAO_ADDR=http://localhost:8300 bao auth enable userpass\n    Success! Enabled userpass auth method at: userpass/\n    $ curl -X PUT -H \"X-Vault-Request: true\" -H \"X-Vault-Token: root\" -d '{\"ttl\":\"asdf\"}' \"http://localhost:8200/v1/auth/userpass/users/asdf\"\n    \n    --> server logs:\n    \n    2025-06-25T21:32:25.101-0500 [ERROR] core: failed to run existence check: error=\"error converting input for field \\\"ttl\\\": time: invalid duration \\\"asdf\\\"\"\n    ```\n    \n    ### Impact\n    \n    This is an information disclosure bug with little mitigation. See\n    https://discuss.hashicorp.com/t/hcsec-2025-09-vault-may-expose-sensitive-information-in-error-logs-when-processing-malformed-data-with-the-kv-v2-plugin/74717\n    for a previous version. That version was fixed, but this is in the\n    second part of that error message (starting at `'' expected a map, got\n    'string'` -- when the field type is `string` and a `map` is provided, we\n    see the above information leak -- the previous example had a `map` type\n    field with a `string` value provided).\n    \n    This was rated 4.5 Medium by HashiCorp in the past iteration.\n    \n    ---\n    \n    ### mapstructure May Leak Sensitive Information in Logs When Processing\n    Malformed Data\n    \n    [GHSA-fv92-fjc5-jj9h](https://redirect.github.com/advisories/GHSA-fv92-fjc5-jj9h)\n    / [GO-2025-3787](https://pkg.go.dev/vuln/GO-2025-3787)\n    \n    <details>\n    <summary>More information</summary>\n    \n    #### Details\n    ##### Summary\n    \n    Use of this library in a security-critical context may result in leaking\n    sensitive information, if used to process sensitive fields.\n    \n    ##### Details\n    \n    OpenBao (and presumably HashiCorp Vault) have surfaced error messages\n    from `mapstructure` as follows:\n    \n    \n    https://github.com/openbao/openbao/blob/98c3a59c040efca724353ca46ca79bd5cdbab920/sdk/framework/field_data.go#L43-L50\n    \n    ```go\n                            _, _, err := d.getPrimitive(field, schema)\n                            if err != nil {\n                                    return fmt.Errorf(\"error converting input for field %q: %w\", field, err)\n                            }\n    ```\n    \n    where this calls `mapstructure.WeakDecode(...)`:\n    https://github.com/openbao/openbao/blob/98c3a59c040efca724353ca46ca79bd5cdbab920/sdk/framework/field_data.go#L181-L193\n    \n    ```go\n    \n    func (d *FieldData) getPrimitive(k string, schema *FieldSchema) (interface{}, bool, error) {\n            raw, ok := d.Raw[k]\n            if !ok {\n                    return nil, false, nil\n            }\n    \n            switch t := schema.Type; This enables\n    frameworks built on top of Ginkgo to modify Ginkgo nodes and enforce\n    conventions.\n    \n    Learn more\n    [here](https://onsi.github.io/ginkgo/#advanced-transforming-node-arguments-during-tree-construction).\n    \n    ##### Spec Prioritization\n    \n    A new `SpecPriority(int)` decorator has been added. Ginkgo will honor\n    priority when ordering specs, ensuring that higher priority specs start\n    running before lower priority specs\n    \n    Learn more [here](https://onsi.github.io/ginkgo/#prioritizing-specs).\n    \n    ##### Maintenance\n    \n    - Bump rexml from 3.4.0 to 3.4.2 in /docs\n    ([#&#8203;1595](https://redirect.github.com/onsi/ginkgo/issues/1595))\n    \\[[`1333dae`](https://redirect.github.com/onsi/ginkgo/commit/1333dae)]\n    - Bump github.com/gkampitakis/go-snaps from 0.5.14 to 0.5.15\n    ([#&#8203;1600](https://redirect.github.com/onsi/ginkgo/issues/1600))\n    \\[[`17ae63e`](https://redirect.github.com/onsi/ginkgo/commit/17ae63e)]\n    \n    ###\n    [`v2.26.0`](https://redirect.github.com/onsi/ginkgo/releases/tag/v2.26.0)\n    \n    [Compare\n    Source](https://redirect.github.com/onsi/ginkgo/compare/v2.25.3...v2.26.0)\n    \n    #### 2.26.0\n    \n    ##### Features\n    \n    Ginkgo can now generate json-formatted reports that are compatible with\n    the `go test` json format. Use `ginkgo --gojson-report=report.go.json`.\n    This is not intended to be a replacement for Ginkgo's native json format\n    which is more information rich and better models Ginkgo's test structure\n    semantics.\n    \n    ###\n    [`v2.25.3`](https://redirect.github.com/onsi/ginkgo/releases/tag/v2.25.3)\n    \n    [Compare\n    Source](https://redirect.github.com/onsi/ginkgo/compare/v2.25.2...v2.25.3)\n    \n    #### 2.25.3\n    \n    ##### Fixes\n    \n    - fix(types): ignore nameless nodes on FullText()\n    \\[[`10866d3`](https://redirect.github.com/onsi/ginkgo/commit/10866d3)]\n    - chore: fix some CodeQL warnings\n    \\[[`2e42cff`](https://redirect.github.com/onsi/ginkgo/commit/2e42cff)]\n    \n    ###\n    [`v2.25.0`](https://redirect.github.com/onsi/ginkgo/releases/tag/v2.25.0)\n    \n    [Compare\n    Source](https://redirect.github.com/onsi/ginkgo/compare/v2.24.0...v2.25.0)\n    \n    #### 2.25.0\n    \n    ##### `AroundNode`\n    \n    This release introduces a new decorator to support more complex spec\n    setup usecases.\n    \n    `AroundNode` registers a function that runs before each individual node.\n    This is considered a more advanced decorator.\n    \n    Please read the\n    [docs](https://onsi.github.io/ginkgo/#advanced-around-node) for more\n    information and some examples.\n    \n    Allowed signatures:\n    \n    - `AroundNode(func())` - `func` will be called before the node is run.\n    - `AroundNode(func(ctx context.Context) context.Context)` - `func` can\n    wrap the passed in context and return a new one which will be passed on\n    to the node.\n    - `AroundNode(func(ctx context.Context, body func(ctx\n    context.Context)))` - `ctx` is the context for the node and `body` is a\n    function that must be called to run the node. This gives you complete\n    control over what runs before and after the node.\n    \n    Multiple `AroundNode` decorators can be applied to a single node and\n    they will run in the order they are applied.\n    \n    Unlike setup nodes like `BeforeEach` and `DeferCleanup`, `AroundNode` is\n    guaranteed to run in the same goroutine as the decorated node. This is\n    necessary when working with lower-level libraries that must run on a\n    single thread (you can call `runtime.LockOSThread()` in the `AroundNode`\n    to ensure that the node runs on a single thread).\n    \n    Since `AroundNode` allows you to modify the context you can also use\n    `AroundNode` to implement shared setup that attaches values to the\n    context.\n    \n    If applied to a container, `AroundNode` will run before every node in\n    the container. Including setup nodes like `BeforeEach` and\n    `DeferCleanup`.\n    \n    `AroundNode` can also be applied to `RunSpecs` to run before every node\n    in the suite. This opens up new mechanisms for instrumenting individual\n    nodes across an entire suite.\n    \n    ###\n    [`v2.24.0`](https://redirect.github.com/onsi/ginkgo/releases/tag/v2.24.0)\n    \n    [Compare\n    Source](https://redirect.github.com/onsi/ginkgo/compare/v2.23.4...v2.24.0)\n    \n    #### 2.24.0\n    \n    ##### Features\n    \n    Specs can now be decorated with (e.g.) `SemVerConstraint(\"2.1.0\")` and\n    `ginkgo --sem-ver-filter=\"2.1.1\"` will only run constrained specs that\n    match the requested version.
